=== Theorem 10.2 hit 1 ===
               0

Fix ε > 0. Then
                                      e−(m−ε)t µF ([0, m − ε]) ≤ CF e−mt .
Multiplication by e(m−ε)t and t → ∞ gives µF ([0, m − ε]) = 0. Letting ε ↓ 0 proves (167).

Theorem 9.3 (Unique vacuum and complete local mass gap). The OS Hamiltonian satisfies

                                               H Ω⊥ ≥ m > 0.                                          (169)

The vacuum is unique.
Proof. The centered classes [F ] − S(F )Ω, F ∈ D+ , are dense in Ω⊥ . Lemma 9.2 says that the spectral
projection EH ([0, m − ε]) annihilates this dense set for every ε > 0; hence it vanishes on Ω⊥ . This proves
the absence of spectrum in (0, m).
   For a centered core vector, (168) tends to zero, so its spectral measure has no atom at zero. Density
then shows that EH ({0})H = CΩ. Thus the vacuum is unique and (169) holds.

10     Nontriviality and finite spectral window
10.1     Endpoint interpolation and reflected noncollapse
Let Yr (f ) be the tangent-normalized, Wick-centered scalar curvature source at physical scale r, with the
source root parallel transported in the normalized finite-jet frame. The test profile f is chosen once at
unit scale and dilated with the source so that its dimensionless support remains fixed and lies strictly in
positive Euclidean time.
Lemma 10.1 (Positive Gaussian OS norm). There is such a profile f for which

                                    V0 (f ) := S0 ΘYr (f ) Yr (f ) > 0.
                                                                  


The value is independent of r in the normalized frame.
Proof. At the Gaussian fixed point the centered scalar curvature composite creates a two-curvature Fock
vector. In Fourier variables its squared norm is an integral of the squared symmetrized two-particle
amplitude against the positive transverse curvature spectral density. That density is nonzero on an open
set, so choosing a positive-time profile whose Fourier transform is nonzero there gives a strictly positive
integral. Reflection positivity identifies the integral with the displayed OS form. Scale covariance of the
tangent-normalized source makes the value independent of r.

  (iv) the no-crossing terminal remainder tends to zero through two g-derivatives uniformly as M → ∞.

same diagonal argument, and convergence of the finitely many Schwinger functions passes a uniform
finite-cutoff lower bound to the limit.

11     Completion for arbitrary fixed compact simple gauge groups

G and faithful unitary representation ρ, the group register CG is finite and all hypotheses consumed by
Sections 2–10 are satisfied. Consequently Theorem 1.1 holds.
Proof. Compactness and the analytic bi-invariant geometry give the Karcher, logarithm, coarea, and
faithful-action constants of Section 2. The complete folded block symbol is spacetime-only and tensors
with Ig , so the strict right-inverse and optical coercivity constants are unchanged. The chart complement
is charged by the faithful Wilson action and the irregular activity is exponentially small. The permanent
weak map, centered graph, and BPHZL packet use the same finite-dimensional Lie-algebra tensor calculus,
with the universal color factor CA (G).
   The Peter–Weyl spectral sum converges at the fixed order sG , and the higher hybrid-polydisc argument
supplies the one-pivot nuclear and multipivot Fourier–Wiener rows. The ordinary-periodic toron, seam,
reflection, and wrapping templates are admitted by Theorem D.3; no twisted sector or finite-volume bundle
sum is used. The invariant-jet theorem supplies the complete low-dimensional mixing and dimension-six
regulator spaces. The joint Haar–optical forest, source transport, Ward hierarchy, OS reconstruction,
and noncollapse theorem then apply with constants depending on G but uniform in cutoff, volume, and
directed depth. Appendix F, specifically Table 3, records every independent entry of CG , the first proof
call at which its G-dependence enters, and the subsequent consumers. Thus no additional estimate
uniform in G is hidden in this completion step: after those finite calls, each remaining mechanism is
color-free, tensors with Ig , or takes a finite maximum in a registered finite-dimensional space.

        cancellation assumption.

A     Endpoint packet-shell verification

   The divided packet has no reflection-even scalar or rank-two local component of engineering dimension

observation will be used only for stable membership, not to estimate the nonlocal packet.

The coefficient of every contact is exactly one. Repeated differentiation gives the complete contact hierarchy,

Proof. At finite cutoff, compact-Haar integration by parts for the globally smooth clover vector field gives

Proposition 8.36 (Noncollapse and canonical normalization). The stress Schwinger distribution is
nonzero. Its normalization is fixed uniquely by the unit contact coefficient in (160); no independent finite
factor is available.
Proof. The reflected curvature source of Theorem 10.5 (proved independently in Section 10) defines
a nonzero centered local vector. Common physical clustering gives its time-translation correlation a
finite positive spectral measure supported away from zero; hence that correlation is not constant and its
distributional translation derivative is nonzero for suitable separated test profiles. Choose v supported
near one insertion. The right side of (160) is then nonzero, so the tensor insertion on the left cannot
vanish. Multiplying the tensor by a finite constant would multiply the left contact coefficient but not the
geometric derivation on the right, forcing the constant to be one.

Result and local-field realization
Theorem 8.37 (Stress/Ward construction). The global displacement theorem plus the projective construc-
tion above produce a nonzero tempered Euclidean tensor insertion on the bounded geometric Schwinger
algebra, with exact translation contacts and signed-hypercubic covariance. The later angular Ward theorem
promotes its continuum multiplet to O(4) covariance.
8.2.5 Euclidean rotation covariance
Low-dimensional rotation-defect classification.
Lemma 8.38 (No low-dimensional gauge-invariant vector). For every compact simple G there is no
nonzero local, reflection-compatible, gauge-invariant vector source of engineering dimension at most four,
modulo vacuum terms and total derivatives.
Proof. A connection mass term is not gauge invariant. At dimension two, Tr dρ(Fµν ) = 0 by semisimplicity.
At dimension three, traces of dρ(DF ) reduce to derivatives of Tr dρ(F ) and vanish. A dimension-four
gauge-invariant vector cannot be formed from two curvature two-forms using only the metric while leaving
one free index; an epsilon tensor would have the wrong reflection parity. Total derivatives are excluded
by the stated quotient.


                                                         86
Lemma 8.39 (Antisymmetric stress defect begins at dimension six). A local signed-hypercubic, reflection-
even, gauge-invariant antisymmetric rank-two source has no component of engineering dimension four or
five. Its first possible nonzero continuum jet has dimension at least six.
Proof. At dimension four the quadratic curvature tensors are generated by Tr(Fµα Fνα ), δµν Tr F 2 , and
the corresponding dual-curvature contractions. The first is symmetric; the dual contraction is proportional
to a diagonal pseudoscalar tensor; none has an antisymmetric part. Reflection symmetry excludes the
odd-derivative dimension-five sector.
Corollary 8.40 (Vanishing rotation regulator). In the projective stress Ward identity, the antisymmetric
regulator insertion is a2 times a normalized family of dimension-six roots. The marked source bounds
therefore make it vanish in the zero-spacing limit. The cutoff rotation Ward identities integrate to full O(4)
covariance once the already established translation, reflection, and tensor-source continuity statements are
inserted.

11     Completion for arbitrary fixed compact simple gauge groups
Theorem 11.1 (Completion of the fixed-group construction). For every fixed compact connected simple
G and faithful unitary representation ρ, the group register CG is finite and all hypotheses consumed by
Sections 2–10 are satisfied. Consequently Theorem 1.1 holds.
Proof. Compactness and the analytic bi-invariant geometry give the Karcher, logarithm, coarea, and
faithful-action constants of Section 2. The complete folded block symbol is spacetime-only and tensors
with Ig , so the strict right-inverse and optical coercivity constants are unchanged. The chart complement
is charged by the faithful Wilson action and the irregular activity is exponentially small. The permanent
weak map, centered graph, and BPHZL packet use the same finite-dimensional Lie-algebra tensor calculus,
with the universal color factor CA (G).
   The Peter–Weyl spectral sum converges at the fixed order sG , and the higher hybrid-polydisc argument
supplies the one-pivot nuclear and multipivot Fourier–Wiener rows. The ordinary-periodic toron, seam,
reflection, and wrapping templates are admitted by Theorem D.3; no twisted sector or finite-volume bundle
sum is used. The invariant-jet theorem supplies the complete low-dimensional mixing and dimension-six
regulator spaces. The joint Haar–optical forest, source transport, Ward hierarchy, OS reconstruction,
and noncollapse theorem then apply with constants depending on G but uniform in cutoff, volume, and
directed depth. Appendix F, specifically Table 3, records every independent entry of CG , the first proof
call at which its G-dependence enters,

covariance remaining after the weak flow is stopped at scale r on a periodic torus of side L ≥ 4r. For

                           |∇j Cr(L) (x, y)| ≤ Cj r−2−j (1 + dL (x, y)/r)−(2+j) ,

Lemma A.3 (Exact even-contact division of complete packets). For every complete packet class λ,
the one-open-leg coefficient vanishes by global color invariance. The renormalized two-open-leg packet is
reflection even, vanishes at p = 0, and admits the exact second-order division
                                                      X
                                    Tλ (p) − Tλ (0) =    pbα pbβ Sλ,αβ (p).                        (188)
                                                       α,β

For every 0 < a′ < a0 , the rooted norm of Sλ is bounded by the second support moment of the same
complete packet at exponent a0 .
   If the complete packet is represented at weak scale i, the two displayed difference factors carry the
canonical relative factor
                                             (r/ℓi )2 = 2−2i                                       (189)
with respect to the fixed physical source scale r. This factor belongs to the whole packet. It is not assigned
to a chosen branch or attachment cut.
Proof. The selector is inserted only after the exact normalized packet is formed and is scalar under color,
translation and aligned reflection. Hence every selected sum is again a complete packet. Its one-open-leg
coefficient is an invariant adjoint vector and therefore vanishes. Global contact subtraction is allocated
after the disjoint packet decomposition, so each selected renormalized coefficient vanishes at p = 0.
Reflection gives Tλ (−p) = Tλ (p) and therefore removes the linear Taylor term. The integral Taylor
formula, or equivalently finite Laurent telescoping in the variables eipα , gives (188). Two momentum
derivatives insert two support displacements, absorbed by the unused exponent a0 − a′ .
   The divided packet has no reflection-even scalar or rank-two local component of engineering dimension
four or five. Lemmas 5.22 and 5.23 therefore place its local projection in the stable degree-six block; this
observation will be used only for stable membership, not to estimate the nonlocal packet.
   The scale factor in (189) is instead purely geometric. In the tangent-normalized profile coordinates, one
lattice difference at representation scale ℓi = 2i r is (r/ℓi ) times the corresponding unit-scale difference.
The two explicit factors in (188) therefore contribute exactly (r/ℓi )2 = 2−2i . The two support moments
of Sλ are absorbed by the unused rooted exponent. This argument applies to the whole packet, local and
nonlocal together. Since the division identity holds for the entire C 2 family, zero, one or two g derivatives
do not remove the two explicit differences.

be the tangent-normalized, Wick-centered scalar curvature source at physical scale r, with the
source root parallel transported in the normalized finite-jet frame. The test profile f is chosen once at

Lemma 10.1 (Positive Gaussian OS norm). There is such a profile f for which

                                    V0 (f ) := S0 ΘYr (f ) Yr (f ) > 0.
                                                                  


The value is independent of r in the normalized frame.
Proof. At the Gaussian fixed point the centered scalar curvature composite creates a two-curvature Fock
vector. In Fourier variables its squared norm is an integral of the squared symmetrized two-particle
amplitude against the positive transverse curvature spectral density. That density is nonzero on an open
set, so choosing a positive-time profile whose Fourier transform is nonzero there gives a strictly positive
integral. Reflection positivity identifies the integral with the displayed OS form. Scale covariance of the
tangent-normalized source makes the value independent of r.

Theorem 10.2 (Uniform endpoint interpolation master theorem). Fix the positive-time profile f and a

each complete BRS/reflection packet is collected. The graph is C 3 in u, so g 7→ (g 2 , R⋆ (g 2 )) is C 2 at

analytic in the physical field A = ga. After the Gaussian quadratic form is removed, a Wilson term of field

partners and coordinate Jacobians are collected before the grading is read, so BRS and reflection relations
are retained. No C 1 statement in the squared coupling u is used. Finite-volume Gaussian equivalence and

Theorem 10.4 (Endpoint packet-shell supplier). For the graph-segment family constructed above, the

    (i) The grounded periodic covariance has volume-independent AA, AF , and F F rows of orders two,

        derivatives are taken before the labelled large-field partition and before bad-core ownership. The
        differentiated partition is then recollected with the same charged events and with no same-owner
        cancellation assumption.
 (iii) Before crossing, the full rooted stable map, including wrapping and charged-flat coordinates, is C 2
        in g with a strictly contracting same-order marked row. At first crossing, the fresh marked family
        lies in a uniform hybrid pivot–jet polydisc and satisfies the original-link Fourier–Wiener incidence
        row required by the joint Haar–optical collect.
  (iv) The crossing contribution and the no-crossing terminal contribution are controlled by one geometri-
        cally summable conditional-packet shell majorant. The same majorant controls the functional and
        its first two g derivatives, extends to the closed crossing cells, and sends the terminal remainder to
        zero uniformly as the terminal depth tends to infinity.
Consequently the complete normalized two-root functional, with or without one fixed dimension-six defect
root, has a cutoff-, volume-, crossing-cell-, and terminal-depth-independent C 2 bound on 0 ≤ g ≤ gr .

entrance estimates; and the terminal-shell summation. No additional mathematical input is imported
beyond the results proved in Sections 3–7.

its strict threshold; marked objects are finite forcing vertices. The complete canonical reported hull, not
a selected connector, carries the remaining exponential weight. This proves (235); (236) is its immediate
corollary.

10     Nontriviality and finite spectral window
10.1     Endpoint interpolation and reflected noncollapse
Let Yr (f ) be the tangent-normalized, Wick-centered scalar curvature source at physical scale r, with the
source root parallel transported in the normalized finite-jet frame. The test profile f is chosen once at
unit scale and dilated with the source so that its dimensionless support remains fixed and lies strictly in
positive Euclidean time.
Lemma 10.1 (Positive Gaussian OS norm). There is such a profile f for which

                                    V0 (f ) := S0 ΘYr (f ) Yr (f ) > 0.
                                                                  


The value is independent of r in the normalized frame.
Proof. At the Gaussian fixed point the centered scalar curvature composite creates a two-curvature Fock
vector. In Fourier variables its squared norm is an integral of the squared symmetrized two-particle
amplitude against the positive transverse curvature spectral density. That density is nonzero on an open
set, so choosing a positive-time profile whose Fourier transform is nonzero there gives a strictly positive
integral. Reflection positivity identifies the integral with the displayed OS form. Scale covariance of the
tangent-normalized source makes the value independent of r.

10.1     Endpoint interpolation and reflected noncollapse
Let Yr (f ) be the tangent-normalized, Wick-centered scalar curvature source at physical scale r, with the
source root parallel transported in the normalized finite-jet frame. The test profile f is chosen once at
unit scale and dilated with the source so that its dimensionless support remains fixed and lies strictly in
positive Euclidean time.
Lemma 10.1 (Positive Gaussian OS norm). There is such a profile f for which

                                    V0 (f ) := S0 ΘYr (f ) Yr (f ) > 0.
                                                                  


The value is independent of r in the normalized frame.
Proof. At the Gaussian fixed point the centered scalar curvature composite creates a two-curvature Fock
vector. In Fourier variables its squared norm is an integral of the squared symmetrized two-particle
amplitude against the positive transverse curvature spectral density. That density is nonzero on an open
set, so choosing a positive-time profile whose Fourier transform is nonzero there gives a strictly positive
integral. Reflection positivity identifies the integral with the displayed OS form. Scale covariance of the
tangent-normalized source makes the value independent of r.

10.2     Uniform endpoint interpolation
Theorem 10.2 (Uniform endpoint interpolation master theorem). Fix the positive-time profile f and a
                                                   √
physical weak scale r, put ur = u(r) and gr = ur , and use g rather than u = g 2 as the interpolation
parameter. For the complete finite-volume normalized reflected two-root functional, and also with one
fixed finite defect root, the following hold on the full segment 0 ≤ g ≤ gr :
    (i) the graph-segment identities are C 2 and exact before every limit;
   (ii) the stable/wrapping orbit rows, the pre-owner two-mark compact entrance row, and the terminal
        packet-shell row have constants independent of cutoff, volume, preceding weak depth, crossing cell,
        and terminal depth M ;
  (iii) differentiation is performed inside each fixed crossing cell before ownership, never on the crossing
        index, and the resulting total derivatives extend across cell boundaries;
  (iv) the no-crossing terminal remainder tends to zero through two g-derivatives uniformly as M → ∞.




                                                      90
Consequently there is one finite Cf , depending only on the fixed profile and finite source order, such that

                                  sup |Q
                                       b ′′r (g)| ≤ Cf ,          sup |Q
                                                                       b ′r (g)| ≤ Cf g,                 (170)
                              0≤g≤gr                             0≤g≤gr

uniformly in all cutoff and volume parameters. The same bound holds after one finite defect root. The
fixed dyadic r may then be inserted into the countable source algebra before the single common diagonal
extraction.
Proof. The exact graph segment and derivative identity are established immediately below. The weak-orbit,
crossing-cell, pre-owner compact-entrance, and terminal-shell estimates are collected in Theorem 10.4;
its single summable majorant controls the functional and its first two derivatives and includes the cell-
boundary and no-crossing limits. This proves the first estimate in (170). Exact evenness gives Q
                                                                                               b ′ (0) = 0,
                                                                                                 r
and integration gives the second. The common-diagonal statement follows from the compatibility argument
at the end of this subsection.
Lemma 10.3 (Existence of the graph-segment interpolation). After choosing r in the common weak
corridor, the segment 0 ≤ g ≤ gr lies in the same real marked domain. At finite cutoff and volume,
evaluate the exact interaction at (g 2 , R⋆ (g 2 )) and express its quadratic part relative to the fixed g = 0
grounded Gaussian. This gives a real C 2 family Vg,r with positive finite normalization. In fixed Gaussian
coordinates its complete local expansion has the exact grading
                           X
                   Vg,r =      g ν Vn,ν;r ,     ν ≥ max{n − 2, 0},       ν ≡ n (mod 2).                   (171)
                            n,ν

Here n is field degree. The Wilson row attains the lower exponent ν = n − 2; Haar, chart, coarea,
determinant, and graph-return rows carry additional nonnegative powers. The grading is read only after
each complete BRS/reflection packet is collected. The graph is C 3 in u, so g 7→ (g 2 , R⋆ (g 2 )) is C 2 at
g = 0. The Wilson word, Haar density, normal return, coarea, determinant, and finite Schur endpoint are
analytic in the physical field A = ga. After the Gaussian quadratic form is removed, a Wilson term of field
degree n carries g n−2 ; species without the g −2 Wilson prefactor carry higher powers. Analyticity under
A = ga and the even dependence of the graph coefficients on g give the congruence in (171). Gauge/ghost
partners and coordinate Jacobians are collected before the grading is read, so BRS and reflection relations
are retained. No C 1 statement in the squared coupling u is used. Finite-volume Gaussian equivalence and
the common analytic tube give positivity, integrability, and uniform C 2 control in g.

Theorem 10.4 (Endpoint packet-shell supplier). For the graph-segment family constructed above, the
complete twice-marked endpoint expansion has the following properties.
    (i) The grounded periodic covariance has volume-independent AA, AF , and F F rows of orders two,
        three, and four. After the exact global contact is subtracted, every complete reflection/BRS packet
        has two lattice-difference factors and therefore gains (r/ℓi )2 at weak scale i.
   (ii) The exact weak recurrence admits a finite crossing-cell decomposition. On each fixed cell, both g
        derivatives are taken before the labelled large-field partition and before bad-core ownership. The
        differentiated partition is then recollected with the same charged events and with no same-owner
        cancellation assumption.
 (iii) Before crossing, the full rooted stable map, including wrapping and charged-flat coordinates, is C 2
        in g with a strictly contracting same-order marked row. At first crossing, the fresh marked family
        lies in a uniform hybrid pivot–jet polydisc and satisfies the original-link Fourier–Wiener incidence
        row required by the joint Haar–optical collect.
  (iv) The crossing contribution and the no-crossing terminal contribution are controlled by one geometri-
        cally summable conditional-packet shell majorant. The same majorant controls the functional and
        its first two g derivatives, extends to the closed crossing cells, and sends the terminal remainder to
        zero uniformly as the terminal depth tends to infinity.
Consequently the complete normalized two-root functional, with or without one fixed dimension-six defect
root, has a cutoff-, volume-, crossing-cell-, and terminal-depth-independent C 2 bound on 0 ≤ g ≤ gr .
Proof. Appendix A gives the full proof inside this manuscript. Its five subsections establish the grounded-
torus covariance and globally subtracted packet division; the weak crossing recurrence and wrapping rows;
the twice-marked conditional-packet contraction; the pre-owner hybrid-polydisc Wiener and compact-
entrance estimates; and the terminal-shell summation. No additional mathematical input is imported
beyond the results proved in Sections 3–7.

Scale-uniform pullback defect row.            Uniformly for 0 ≤ g ≤ gr ,
                                            ±
                                          ∥Dg,r ∥r,[1] ≤ Cf r2 L(r)pf .                                 (181)

The same row holds after reflection and with every finite passive source mark used in the two-root stability
estimate.
                                      sup ∥Dg,r±
                                                 ∥r,[1] ≤ Cf r2 L(r)pf .                              (182)
                                      0≤g≤gr

The marked first-exit theorem and complementary high-jet source transport both begin two engineering
degrees above the fixed principal target. Their scale-normalized rows are therefore bounded by Cf r2 L(r)pf
uniformly on the common graph segment. This estimate uses no coupling derivative and so requires only
the already proved finite marked source bridge.

Expand the connected two-root form using O± = O b ± + D ± . Every difference term contains a defect root

Compatibility of the physical scale with the common diagonal. Let f be the profile of
Lemma 10.1, and let the constants Cf , pf be those in the uniform endpoint and source-defect rows
above. Choose the fixed countable source-scale set used in Corollary 8.12 to contain a cofinal dyadic
sequence rn ↓ 0. There is an index n such that, for r = rn ,
                                                         1
                                     Cf u(r) + r2 L(r)pf ≤ V0 (f ).
                                       
                                                          2
Before taking the common diagonal subsequence, enlarge the countable source algebra by the fixed fields
Yr (f ), ΘYr (f ), and their reflected product. The same single diagonal extraction then gives all Schwinger
limits entering their OS form and preserves every cutoff- and volume-uniform lower bound. The scale r is
not changed after extraction. The cofinal dyadic choice is countable, while u(r) → 0 and r2 L(r)pf → 0
along it. Hence one r satisfies the displayed inequality. Adding finitely many fields at that scale preserves
countability of the source algebra. The source-tree theorem and Corollary 8.12 therefore permit the
same diagonal argument, and convergence of the finitely many Schwinger functions passes a uniform
finite-cutoff lower bound to the limit.

                                                       95
Lemma A.4 (g-marked one-scale admission). There is g0 > 0 such that, for real 0 ≤ g ≤ g0 , the complete
normalized interaction and all declared source/defect marks are twice continuously differentiable in the
permanent regulator space. An undistinguished non-Gaussian child has parent-normalized row

                                                 η0 (g) ≤ Cg,

while a child carrying one or two distinguished g derivatives has row

                                              η1 (g) + η2 (g) ≤ C.

The exactly recombined large-field/irregular row and its first two g derivatives extend continuously by zero
                                             2
at g = 0 and are bounded by Cm g −pm e−c/g for m ≤ 2. No claim that an attached odd child is O(g 2 ) is
made.
Proof. In the scale-normalized Gaussian coordinate, the cubic Yang–Mills species starts at order g, the
quartic and other non-Gaussian even species start at order g 2 , and the common species tube controls
all field and source marks. Thus an unmarked species row is Cg, while one or two explicit g derivatives
remove at most the corresponding powers and give a finite row. The regulator allocations are additive
under multiplication, and the Gram and Pruefer estimates in the summed replica–BKAR theorem are
insensitive to the parity of an attached tensor. The ordinary parent-normalized child recursion therefore
closes for Cg0 below its printed threshold, with two fixed derivative marks handled by Leibniz. The
C 3 -flat irregular estimate in u = g 2 gives the displayed first and second g-derivative rows after the chain
rule. In particular the model child gA3 differentiated once by a covariance edge is correctly admitted as
an O(g) child, and one g derivative of that child is O(1).
Lemma A.5 (Cellwise complete shell decomposition). Fix the finite directed depth M and one crossing
cell, including the no-crossing cell. After the selected exact factorization has been completed, apply the
canonical reported-support selector only to whole normalized packets. The separated part of the two-root
functional has an exact disjoint decomposition
                                                       X
                                          Qb sep (g) =    FM,k (g)                                   (190)
                                             r,M
                                                       k≥0

whose coefficients are C 2 on the relative interior of that cell. Every normalization, denominator, localiza-
tion, irregular and source-motion contact belongs to exactly one selected packet. No assertion that the
individual coefficients FM,k agree for two different stopping factorizations is needed or made.
Proof. At fixed finite volume and depth, the reporting-telescope and owner-map identities form a disjoint
finite or absolutely convergent inventory of complete normalized packets. Their canonical physical reported
supports therefore admit disjoint scalar dyadic selectors after packet completion. Inside one crossing
cell the stopping depth and exact factorization are fixed, so the graph-segment C 2 identity and the
finite marked forest permit termwise differentiation. Summing the selectors gives the unselected exact
functional, proving (190).
Definition A.6 (Finite directed continuation and crossing cells). Fix the physical weak scale r, its
               √
endpoint gr = ur , and an arbitrary finite further directed depth M . Choose one terminal coarse side
Mout and set Mi = 2M −i Mout . The side is first chosen admissible for Lemma 6.1 and is enlarged once,
independently of M , to satisfy the fixed-torus smallness threshold of Theorem A.9. For every 0 ≤ g ≤ gr ,
use the exact weak recurrence

               u0 (g) = g 2 ,    ui+1 (g) = Fi (ui (g)),   gi (g) = ui (g),   0 ≤ i < M.
                                                                   p

Let
                                  NM (g) := min{0 ≤ i ≤ M : ui (g) ≥ ū},
with NM (g) = ∞ if the set is empty. Uniform outgoing transversality implies that, when NM (g) < ∞,

                               ui (g) < ū   (i < NM (g)),      uNM (g) (g) ∈ I⋆ .

The relative interiors of the sets on which NM (g) = N are called the crossing cells. On such a cell the
exact factorization consists of N weak conditionals followed by the printed finite macrobridge and joint
compact–optical logarithm. On the no-crossing cell it consists of all M weak conditionals followed by the
terminal weak logarithm on the fixed coarse torus.


                                                      96
   This is a choice of exact factorization, not a modification of the measure. The integer NM (g) is never
differentiated. Derivatives are taken inside one crossing cell, where the depth is fixed, and are extended
to cell boundaries using the original finite-volume C 2 normalized functional. At g = 0 one always uses
the no-crossing factorization and later sends M → ∞.
Definition A.7 (Canonical finite-volume off-graph coordinates). For the directed schedule in Defini-
tion A.6, put
                           εi := e−σMi ,     δi := δG (Mi ),    Mi = 2M −i Mout .
The first quantity records genuine wrapping histories in the interaction; the second records the scalar
massless finite-size comparison. After each complete connected output has been formed and assigned its
canonical reported hull, let Piwr select precisely the history certificates containing at least one periodic
wrapping occurrence at some scale not later than i. If Ri is the exact finite-volume centered interaction
and R⋆ (ui ) is the rooted-thermodynamic invariant graph, set

                      Ωi := Ri − R⋆ (ui ),       Wi := Piwr Ωi ,     Bi := (I − Piwr )Ωi .               (191)

Thus Ωi = Wi + Bi coefficientwise. Both Ri and R⋆ (ui ) are written in the same exact schedule-fixed
vacuum and tangent-normalized coupling coordinates. The coordinate intertwining and supportwise
                                    [2]
extraction identity therefore give EX Ωi,X = 0 for every reported support X; all three off-graph coordinates
lie in the actual centered stable fiber. The first coordinate is the canonical sum of histories carrying a
wrapping certificate. After the tangent-normalized scalar coordinate is extracted, the massless acoustic
sum–integral correction belongs to the nonautonomous scalar map Fi and is not an off-graph activity.
By the exact regular interaction comparison and label recombination, the only nonwrapping off-graph
difference is the slaved U4 charged/atlas complement; this is Bi and is exponentially flat in ui . No flat
charged coefficient is identified with a wrapping history.

Lemma A.8 (Full rooted finite-torus stable-map comparison). Let Φi (u, R) be the exact scheduled
one-step map on the centered stable interaction after the vacuum, complete two-jet and tangent-normalized

Proof. Use the complete labelled connected-history representation before forgetting owners. In the
scheduled torus and in the rooted thermodynamic step, pair a history whenever its canonically reported
hull has an injective periodic lift and contains no charged atlas label. The local Karcher, Gaussian, coarea,
determinant, localization, extraction and collect templates are then literally the same, so paired histories
and their derivatives cancel coefficientwise. An unpaired regular history has a nontrivial periodic cycle;
its reported hull has length at least cMi . Weakening the permanent rooted support exponent once gives

whose coefficients are C 2 on the relative interior of that cell. Every normalization, denominator, localiza-

Proof. At fixed finite volume and depth, the reporting-telescope and owner-map identities form a disjoint
finite or absolutely convergent inventory of complete normalized packets. Their canonical physical reported
supports therefore admit disjoint scalar dyadic selectors after packet completion. Inside one crossing
cell the stopping depth and exact factorization are fixed, so the graph-segment C 2 identity and the
finite marked forest permit termwise differentiation. Summing the selectors gives the unselected exact
functional, proving (190).
Definition A.6 (Finite directed continuation and crossing cells). Fix the physical weak scale r, its
               √
endpoint gr = ur , and an arbitrary finite further directed depth M . Choose one terminal coarse side
Mout and set Mi = 2M −i Mout . The side is first chosen admissible for Lemma 6.1 and is enlarged once,
independently of M , to satisfy the fixed-torus smallness threshold of Theorem A.9. For every 0 ≤ g ≤ gr ,
use the exact weak recurrence

               u0 (g) = g 2 ,    ui+1 (g) = Fi (ui (g)),   gi (g) = ui (g),   0 ≤ i < M.
                                                                   p

Let
                                  NM (g) := min{0 ≤ i ≤ M : ui (g) ≥ ū},
with NM (g) = ∞ if the set is empty. Uniform outgoing transversality implies that, when NM (g) < ∞,

                               ui (g) < ū   (i < NM (g)),      uNM (g) (g) ∈ I⋆ .

The relative interiors of the sets on which NM (g) = N are called the crossing cells. On such a cell the
exact factorization consists of N weak conditionals followed by the printed finite macrobridge and joint
compact–optical logarithm. On the no-crossing cell it consists of all M weak conditionals followed by the
terminal weak logarithm on the fixed coarse torus.


                                                      96
   This is a choice of exact factorization, not a modification of the measure. The integer NM (g) is never
differentiated. Derivatives are taken inside one crossing cell, where the depth is fixed, and are extended
to cell boundaries using the original finite-volume C 2 normalized functional. At g = 0 one always uses
the no-crossing factorization and later sends M → ∞.
Definition A.7 (Canonical finite-volume off-graph coordinates). For the directed schedule in Defini-
tion A.6, put
                           εi := e−σMi ,     δi := δG (Mi ),    Mi = 2M −i Mout .
The first quantity records genuine wrapping histories in the interaction; the second records the scalar
massless finite-size comparison. After each complete connected output has been formed and assigned its
canonical reported hull, let Piwr select precisely the history certificates containing at least one periodic
wrapping occurrence at some scale not later than i. If Ri is the exact finite-volume centered interaction
and R⋆ (ui ) is the rooted-thermodynamic invariant graph, set

                      Ωi := Ri − R⋆ (ui ),       Wi := Piwr Ωi ,     Bi := (I − Piwr )Ωi .               (191)

Thus Ωi = Wi + Bi coefficientwise. Both Ri and R⋆ (ui ) are written in the same exact schedule-fixed
vacuum and tangent-normalized coupling coordinates. The coordinate intertwining and supportwise
                                    [2]
extraction identity therefore give EX Ωi,X = 0 for every reported support X; all three off-graph coordinates
lie in the actual centered stable fiber. The first coordinate is the canonical sum of histories carrying a
wrapping certificate. After the tangent-normalized scalar coordinate is extracted, the massless acoustic
sum–integral correction belongs to the nonautonomous scalar map Fi and is not an off-graph activity.
By the exact regular interaction comparison and label recombination, the only nonwrapping off-graph
difference is the slaved U4 charged/atlas complement; this is Bi and is exponentially flat in ui . No flat
charged coefficient is identified with a wrapping history.

Proof. Use the complete labelled connected-history representation before forgetting owners. In the
scheduled torus and in the rooted thermodynamic step, pair a history whenever its canonically reported
hull has an injective periodic lift and contains no charged atlas label. The local Karcher, Gaussian, coarea,

                                                       97
Passing to the adapted norm is harmless: that norm is a fixed finite sum of rooted norms of powers of the
rooted-thermodynamic linear map, and each power preserves the preceding paired/unpaired classification.
   At R = R⋆ (u), the free term cancels because both maps are written in the same schedule-fixed vacuum
coordinates. The stable tangent uH1 is exponentially local but need not have finite support, so an
unpaired periodic image may already be of order εi u. This sharp row gives (192); no quadratic stable
cancellation is asserted. If the output is projected to the wrapping ideal, a charged history without a
prior periodic certificate must also acquire a new nontrivial periodic cycle. It therefore pays both the
charged flat row and εi+1 . The same observation applied to a nonwrapping distinguished input proves
(194) and (195). These are statements about the full centered interaction map, not inferences from the
scalar comparison.
   Finally differentiate the labelled identities before summing histories. A g mark either stays on the
distinguished input or hits one of finitely many local atoms, the scalar orbit, the extraction projector,
or a new periodic connector. In the first case the same pairing proves the same-order coefficient in the
statement. In every other case the marked child row and the trajectory ledger give the polynomial factor,
while the periodic hull or charged event remains present. This proves (196) through two marks.

strictly inside the reproduced weak ball. Marked rows are distinguished forcing rows; only the unmarked
interaction is used for admission.

A.3     Twice-marked conditional packets and even-contact contraction
Lemma A.10 (Cellwise half-power and two-mark ledger). There are constants Cm and finite exponents
pm such that, uniformly in M , in the crossing cell, and in every index before the selected terminal factor,
                      i                                                         i
                      X                                                         X                 √
                          uh (g) ≤ C log(i + 2),                                      |gh (g)| ≤ C i + 1,      (206)
                       h=0                                                      h=0
        |∂gm ui (g)| + |∂gm gi (g)| ≤ Cm (i + 2)pm ,      m = 0, 1, 2.                                         (207)

The same estimates hold on the no-crossing cell through i = M .
Proof. On a cell with crossing depth N , the reciprocal comparison from Theorem 6.4 gives ui (g) ≤
C(N − i + A)−1 before the crossing. On the no-crossing cell the direct fixed-schedule estimate (201) gives
the same row with N replaced by M ; no extension of the nonautonomous schedule is made. Summation
gives (206).


                                                           99
   For crossing cells use the printed marked C 3 orbit row. For the no-crossing cell use (199). In either
case every Fi fixes zero, has positive derivative, and can be written as Fi (u) = uϕi (u) with ϕi positive and
uniformly C 2 . Its finite composition therefore factors as ui (g) = g 2 ψi (g 2 ) with ψi > 0. Differentiating
the exact recurrence twicep and using Fi (ui ) ≤
                                           ′
                                                    1 + Cui and |Fi′′ (ui )| ≤ C gives polynomial growth in i.
Consequently gi (g) = g ψi (g ) is an odd C function and no 1/g singularity occurs at the Gaussian
                                2                2

endpoint. The interaction marks required by the finite conditional forest are simultaneously controlled by
(203) and (204). All constants are uniform on the compact first-crossing interval and on the enlarged
no-crossing weak wedge.
Lemma A.11 (Exact finite-depth twice-marked conditional forest). At finite directed depth M , differen-
tiate the exact factorization of Definition A.6 with respect to the two curvature roots and at most two
copies of g. The result is a finite sum of complete scale-labelled packet trees such that:
    (i) the two source leaves have one unique lowest common ancestor;
   (ii) each g mark is owned exactly once by a trajectory coordinate, a conditional logarithm, a source
        return, the terminal weak logarithm, or the compact handoff logarithm;
  (iii) every covariance edge, hard-core derivative, field factorial, regulator allocation and normalization
        contact belongs to one complete one-step packet before multiscale composition;
  (iv) the coefficients are exactly those of the Banach-valued Faà di Bruno formula.
No infinite-depth passage is asserted in this lemma.
Proof. Apply the joint-cumulant derivative identity to every exact normalized weak conditional and, on
a crossing cell, to the exact finite macrobridge and joint compact–optical logarithm. Then apply the
Banach-valued Faà di Bruno formula to the finite composition. This assertion is coefficientwise and uses
only finite-volume differentiability; the uniform compact marked bound is supplied later by Theorem A.20.
The one-step summed replica–BKAR theorem controls each weak packet, Lemma A.4 controls zero,
one and two g marks, and finite source transport controls the external roots. Uniqueness of the lowest
common ancestor is the ordinary tree property.
Lemma A.12 (Top marked ancestor and complete-packet shell). In a packet tree from Lemma A.11,
contract every maximal descendant subtree carrying no distinguished label into its exact parent-normalized
decoration, and suppress every maximal unary chain into its exact ordered transfer product. The distin-
guished labels are the two curvature roots and the zero, one or two active g marks. They have a unique
lowest common ancestor v⊤ ; let j be its conditional scale. Call v⊤ the top marked ancestor.
    All vertices strictly above v⊤ are unmarked. The complete coefficient created at v⊤ includes every
contracted side decoration with its canonical reported support. Decompose these whole coefficients by their
full reported-support shell k using a scalar translation- and reflection-invariant selector. The selected
class is therefore a complete BRS/reflection packet with every denominator and normalization contact
still inside it.
    If j ≤ k + C0 , the permanent rooted-support row of that whole packet gives
                                                                 k−j
                                            C(1 + 2k−j )q e−c2         .                                   (208)

If j > k + C0 , the full support lies inside a fixed enlargement of one scale-j cell and no spatial connector
estimate is needed.

Proof. Contraction of an unlabelled descendant is its exact conditional value, so it retains its canonical
reported support. Suppression of a unary chain is the exact ordered transfer product. After these
operations, a tree with at most four distinguished leaves has only a fixed number of essential branching
edges, while all arbitrarily long unary portions are already contained in the pa

reported support. Suppression of a unary chain is the exact ordered transfer product. After these
operations, a tree with at most four distinguished leaves has only a fixed number of essential branching
edges, while all arbitrarily long unary portions are already contained in the parity transfer product. The
unique lowest common ancestor of all labels is an ordinary tree invariant. If a g derivative lands on a
coarser conditional map, its mark is attached at that map and therefore moves v⊤ to that scale. Hence
no marked vertex can occur above v⊤ .
   The shell selector is applied only after the full packet at v⊤ has been collected. A remote unmarked
side decoration is not cut from its parent and is not asked to carry an external Taylor factor. If exact
localization turns its contribution into a smaller-hull contact, that term is already in the finite contact block
and is not a shell-k packet. Otherwise its canonical reported support remains part of the whole packet.
When that support spans 2k−j scale-j blocks, the strict rooted-support norm and the weak connector
theorem give (208), including covariance, hard-core and mixed alternatives. The exact Loc/collect identity
of Theorem 5.8 ensures that this dichotomy is made before the stable packet is declared.

Lemma A.11 (Exact finite-depth twice-marked conditional forest). At finite directed depth M , differen-
tiate the exact factorization of Definition A.6 with respect to the two curvature roots and at most two
copies of g. The result is a finite sum of complete scale-labelled packet trees such that:
    (i) the two source leaves have one unique lowest common ancestor;
   (ii) each g mark is owned exactly once by a trajectory coordinate, a conditional logarithm, a source
        return, the terminal weak logarithm, or the compact handoff logarithm;
  (iii) every covariance edge, hard-core derivative, field factorial, regulator allocation and normalization
        contact belongs to one complete one-step packet before multiscale composition;
  (iv) the coefficients are exactly those of the Banach-valued Faà di Bruno formula.
No infinite-depth passage is asserted in this lemma.
Proof. Apply the joint-cumulant derivative identity to every exact normalized weak conditional and, on
a crossing cell, to the exact finite macrobridge and joint compact–optical logarithm. Then apply the
Banach-valued Faà di Bruno formula to the finite composition. This assertion is coefficientwise and uses
only finite-volume differentiability; the uniform compact marked bound is supplied later by Theorem A.20.
The one-step summed replica–BKAR theorem controls each weak packet, Lemma A.4 controls zero,
one and two g marks, and finite source transport controls the external roots. Uniqueness of the lowest
common ancestor is the ordinary tree property.

Theorem 10.4 (Endpoint packet-shell supplier). For the graph-segment family constructed above, the
complete twice-marked endpoint expansion has the following properties.
    (i) The grounded periodic covariance has volume-independent AA, AF , and F F rows of orders two,
        three, and four. After the exact global contact is subtracted, every complete reflection/BRS packet
        has two lattice-difference factors and therefore gains (r/ℓi )2 at weak scale i.
   (ii) The exact weak recurrence admits a finite crossing-cell decomposition. On each fixed cell, both g
        derivatives are taken before the labelled large-field partition and before bad-core ownership. The
        differentiated partition is then recollected with the same charged events and with no same-owner
        cancellation assumption.
 (iii) Before crossing, the full rooted stable map, including wrapping and charged-flat coordinates, is C 2
        in g with a strictly contracting same-order marked row. At first crossing, the fresh marked family
        lies in a uniform hybrid pivot–jet polydisc and satisfies the original-link Fourier–Wiener incidence
        row required by the joint Haar–optical collect.
  (iv) The crossing contribution and the no-crossing terminal contribution are controlled by one geometri-
        cally summable conditional-packet shell majorant. The same majorant controls the functional and
        its first two g derivatives, extends to the closed crossing cells, and sends the terminal remainder to
        zero uniformly as the terminal depth tends to infinity.
Consequently the complete normalized two-root functional, with or without one fixed dimension-six defect
root, has a cutoff-, volume-, crossing-cell-, and terminal-depth-independent C 2 bound on 0 ≤ g ≤ gr .
Proof. Appendix A gives the full proof inside this manuscript. Its five subsections establish the grounded-
torus covariance and globally subtracted packet division; the weak crossing recurrence and wrapping rows;
the twice-marked conditional-packet contraction; the pre-owner hybrid-polydisc Wiener and compact-
entrance estimates; and the terminal-shell summation. No additional mathematical input is imported
beyond the results proved in Sections 3–7.

Before taking the common diagonal subsequence, enlarge the countable source algebra by the fixed fields
Yr (f ), ΘYr (f ), and their reflected product. The same single diagonal extraction then gives all Schwinger
limits entering their OS form and preserves every cutoff- and volume-uniform lower bound. The scale r is
not changed after extraction. The cofinal dyadic choice is countable, while u(r) → 0 and r2 L(r)pf → 0
along it. Hence one r satisfies the displayed inequality. Adding finitely many fields at that scale preserves
countability of the source algebra. The source-tree theorem and Corollary 8.12 therefore permit the
same diagonal argument, and convergence of the finitely many Schwinger functions passes a uniform
finite-cutoff lower bound to the limit.

                                            C(1 + 2k−j )q e−c2         .                                   (208)

If j > k + C0 , the full support lies inside a fixed enlargement of one scale-j cell and no spatial connector
estimate is needed.

Proof. Contraction of an unlabelled descendant is its exact conditional value, so it retains its canonical
reported support. Suppression of a unary chain is the exact ordered transfer product. After these
operations, a tree with at most four distinguished leaves has only a fixed number of essential branching
edges, while all arbitrarily long unary portions are already contained in the parity transfer product. The
unique lowest common ancestor of all labels is an ordinary tree invariant. If a g derivative lands on a
coarser conditional map, its mark is attached at that map and therefore moves v⊤ to that scale. Hence
no marked vertex can occur above v⊤ .
   The shell selector is applied only after the full packet at v⊤ has been collected. A remote unmarked
side decoration is not cut from its parent and is not asked to carry an external Taylor factor. If exact
localization turns its contribution into a smaller-hull contact, that term is already in the finite contact block
and is not a shell-k packet. Otherwise its canonical reported support remains part of the whole packet.
When that support spans 2k−j scale-j blocks, the strict rooted-support norm and the weak connector
theorem give (208), including covariance, hard-core and mixed alternatives. The exact Loc/collect identity
of Theorem 5.8 ensures that this dichotomy is made before the stable packet is declared.

Proof. Work first at finite directed depth M and inside one crossing cell of Definition A.6. Apply the
exact marked conditional forest. If the top marked ancestor is a weak conditional vertex of scale j,
Lemma A.14 gives the complete row at that scale. Every later weak transfer before the selected terminal
factor is unmarked, and Theorem A.13 contracts the entire complete rooted packet in one norm. A
coupling mark after the two source roots have joined is not omitted: it becomes another distinguished
leaf and moves the top marked ancestor to its own scale.
   If the top marked ancestor is the selected terminal factor, apply Theorem A.22. On a crossing cell
this is the complete compact handoff packet, with the two differences created at the first-crossing scale
and the remaining separation paid by the joint Haar–optical connector. On the no-crossing cell it is
the finite-depth weak terminal packet on the fixed coarse torus. The latter tends to zero through two
g derivatives as M → ∞. Thus no bridge-free expansion of the residual grounded covariance and no
massless Schur row is required.

therefore pass to the cutoff and volume limits uniformly on that closed cell. At a shared boundary the
original total finite-volume normalized functional is C 2 by Lemma 10.3; equality of the two one-sided
total derivatives passes to the limit. Thus no individual shell coefficients are compared and the crossing
index is never differentiated. At g = 0 use the no-crossing cell for every M and let M → ∞.
   The shell majorant is summable in k. Dominated convergence therefore permits the directed-depth,
volume and ultraviolet limits and two g derivatives, also with one fixed defect root.

its strict threshold; marked objects are finite forcing vertices. The complete canonical reported hull, not
a selected connector, carries the remaining exponential weight. This proves (235); (236) is its immediate
corollary.

A.5     Terminal remainder and geometric packet-shell summation
Lemma A.21 (Fixed-terminal-volume weak packet bound). On the no-crossing cell at directed depth
M , rescale the terminal weak law to the fixed admissible coarse torus. The complete terminal normalized
logarithm, with the two curvature roots, the physical shell mark and at most two g marks, has a uniform



                                                         107
unweighted rooted local-packet norm. After the trajectory and source-return chain rules its row is bounded
by                                                √
                                 Cm (M + 2)P eCm M +1 ,       m = 0, 1, 2.               

=== wrapping hit 1 ===
es a Peter–Weyl Fourier–Wiener landing for the exact one-pivot and
    multipivot densities. Separated Haar pivots contract nonvacuum compact flux, optical covariance
    contracts normal-field stretches, and all remaining vertices retain their support charge. A single
    marked replica–BKAR forest then gives a boundary-to-center two-root connector and one physical
    clustering rate. General invariant-jet transport, displacement and stress Ward identities, finite
    dimension-four mixing, and a dimension-six rotation regulator yield a tempered, reflection-positive,
    translation-continuous, O(4)-covariant Schwinger family. Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction gives a
    positive-energy Poincaré-covariant local theory with unique vacuum and a strictly positive Hamiltonian
    gap. Endpoint-safe curvature-source transport produces a nonzero local vector with positive spectral
    mass in a finite interval above the gap. The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet
    observable theory. No estimate is asserted uniformly over the class of compact simple groups.

1     The compact-simple-group theorem and proof boundary
1.1    Problem and result
The constructive Yang–Mills problem asks for a nontrivial quantum gauge field theory on R4 satisfying
the Osterwalder–Schrader and Wightman requirements and possessing a strictly positive mass gap [1].
The physical scale, thermodynamic limit, zero lattice spacing, Euclidean covariance, local spectral content,
and non-abelian field geometry must be controlled in one compatible construction.
   Fix throughout a compact connected simple Lie group G, a faithful unitary representation ρ : G → U (Vρ )
of dimension dρ , and a positive normalization of the invariant quadratic form for which the adjoint color
factor is denoted CA (G). The Wilson plaquette function is
                                                        1
                                                                    
                                   wG,ρ (U ) = cρ 1 −     Re Tr ρ(U ) ,
                                                       dρ
where cρ > 0 is chosen once so that the Hessian at the identity is the quadratic form used by the
background-field coupling coordinate u = g 2 . This choice is a finite linear normalization of the bare
Wilson coupling and is part of the fixed-group input register.
Theorem 1.1 (Four-dimensional Yang–Mills construction for fixed compact simple G). For every fixed
pair (G, ρ) as above, there is a directed sequence of reflection-positive Wilson lattice measures, with
microscopic spacing tending to zero and periodic volume tending to infinity, whose local gauge-invariant
curvature-jet Schwinger functions converge to a nontrivial Euclidean field theory on R4 .
   The limiting Schwinger family is tempered, permutation symmetric, reflection positive, translation
continuous, exponentially clustering, and O(4) covariant. Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction yields a
positive-energy Poincaré-covariant local theory with a unique vacuum and a number m⋆ (G) > 0 such that
                                              H Ω⊥ ≥ m⋆ (G).
On the centered invariant weak trajectory, the exact scale-two coupling map is
                                            11 CA (G) log 2
                           u(2L) = u(L) +                   u(L)2 + OG (u(L)3 ),
                                                 24π 2
with uniform volume and cutoff control in the permanent weak regime. The reconstructed theory contains
a nonzero reflected local curvature vector and positive spectral mass in a finite interval [m⋆ (G), M⋆ (G)].
1.2    Fixed-group conventions and finite register
Write g = Lie(G) and dG = dim G. All Haar measures are normalized. The finite-volume approximants
use the ordinary periodic link space GE(Λ) with periodic gauge transformations; no twisted boundary
condition or nontrivial finite-volume bundle sector is summed. This is sufficient for the claimed local
theory on the contractible space R4 . The bi-invariant metric, exponential coordinates, Laplacian ∆G ,
Casimir eigenvalues, and operator norms are those determined by the declared quadratic form. In
curvature formulas, Tr(XY ) denotes this normalized invariant bilinear pairing; raw representation traces
are written Tr ρ(·). Let                     
                                              dG                    dG
                                      sG :=       + 1,      2sG >       .
                                               4                      2
The fixed-group constant register CG consists of the finite quantities used below: the injectivity and
convexity radii, a positive local Karcher radius, finite holomorphic BCH and logarithm bounds through
order 2sG + 3, the faithful-action Hessian and complement charge, the Peter–Weyl constant, the finite
ordinary-periodic toron/seam template register, the adjoint color factor, and the finite invariant-jet
dimensions through the retained degree. Every statement called uniform is uniform in cutoff, volume,
directed depth, periodic wrapping, admitted exterior histories, and the finite reflected template family,
but its constants may depend on CG .

Proposition 1.2 (Reflection-positive Wilson input). For every finite periodic lattice Λ and u > 0, in the
ordinary periodic link sector,
                                                "                    #
                             G,ρ
                                                       X                Y
                                        −1
                          dµΛ,u (U ) = ZΛ,u exp −u  −1
                                                           wG,ρ (Up )     dmG (Ue )
                                                         p             e

is gauge invariant and reflection positive for coordinate reflections. Moreover wG,ρ ≥ 0, its zero set is the
identity, and on one fixed identity neighborhood
                                     cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2 .


                                                     3
Proof. The normalized character satisfies Re Tr ρ(U ) ≤ dρ , with equality only when ρ(U ) = I; faithfulness
gives U = e. The Hessian statement follows from the chosen normalization and compactness. Up to a field-
independent constant, each plaquette factor is exp{t(χρ +χρ )} with t > 0. Its power series is a nonnegative
linear combination of characters of tensor products of ρ and ρ̄, and their irreducible decompositions
have nonnegative integer multiplicities. The standard character-expansion reflection-positivity argument
therefore applies.

1.3     Proof architecture
The proof has four stages. First, the exact Karcher block is placed in a complete full-alias normal chart
and combined with a charged chart-complement partition, permanent analytic domain, marked connected
logarithm, Wick-stable jet block, and centered invariant graph. The folded spacetime symbol tensors
with Ig , while all nonlinear estimates are admitted by the finite register CG .
   Second, exact background-scheme conjugacy identifies the universal marginal coefficient      and a directed
schedule reaches the compact matching scale. The higher product Sobolev operator p (1 − ∆p )sG replaces
                                                                                       Q
the rank-one order and supplies the fixed-G Fourier–Wiener rows.
   Third, complete Haar and optical variables are kept in one partition function. A mixed replica–
BKAR forest makes every root-to-root path pay separated Haar contraction, optical covariance decay,
or pre-existing support charge. Ordinary-periodic toron backgrounds, seams, reflections, and wrapping
certificates enter only through the finite template theorem of Appendix D.
   Finally, a least-label source forest transports the complete invariant-jet system. The degree-four block
is group-independent because a compact simple Lie algebra has a unique invariant symmetric bilinear
form; possible cubic color tensors first enter the finite dimension-six block. Ward identities, rotation-defect
estimates, reflection-positive recombination, and Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction give covariance,
the unique vacuum, the gap, and noncollapse.
1.4     External inputs and exact proof boundary
Only the theorem-level inputs in Table 1 are imported. The fixed-group geometry, action charge, higher
Sobolev landing, periodic-toron template theorem, invariant-jet admission, matching constants, and
noncollapse source are internal.

1.5     Self-contained relation to the rank-one construction
The companion SU(2) manuscript [14] is a completely expanded rank-one specialization and a useful
independent check. It is not a logical input to Theorem 1.1: every common analytic theorem used here is
stated and proved in this paper, and every new fixed-group admission is printed below. Appendix F gives
a theorem-level specialization map so that a referee can identify which arguments are color-free, which
tensor with Ig , and which require new group data.
1.6     Scope
The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet observable sector for each fixed compact
connected simple G. It does not assert a bound uniform over groups, a simultaneous large-rank limit,
control of every extended Wilson-line or generalized-symmetry sector, or uniqueness of the continuum
limit outside the directed construction. Quotient groups are included through their actual compact link
group. The construction uses only the ordinary periodic finite-volume sector and retains all flat toron
backgrounds occurring in that sector; no claim about a sum over twisted boundary conditions is made or
needed.

2     Fixed-group Wilson geometry and Karcher blocking
Let Λ be a finite periodic four-dimensional lattice and use the reflection-positive Wilson measure of
Proposition 1.2. For each oriented retained link, use the straight two-link path with weight 7/8 and the
forty-eight signed-corner staples with weight 1/384 each. On a fixed strongly convex identity tube, define
the retained link as their weighted Karcher mean in the bi-invariant metric.

Proof. For v = a − b, integrate the derivative along the segment from b to a. Schur’s test gives

and using its Neumann series.
Theorem 3.4 (Finite-cell retention and chart charge). There are constants Cret < ∞, cch > 0, and
tch > 0, depending only on the fixed cell atlas, such that

                                            DQ (U ) ≤ Cret AQ+ (U )

for every fine configuration. Moreover,

                                   TQ (U ) ≥ tch /4     =⇒     AQ+ (U ) ≥ cch .

The constants are independent of total volume, ultraviolet depth, periodic wrapping, and the aligned-
reflection template.
Proof. Use a boundary-rooted tree gauge on the fixed finite contractible cell. By Lemma 3.1, the zero set
of AQ+ is precisely the flat set. On that set all common-endpoint paths agree, hence DQ = TQ = 0. Near
the flat set, the representation-normalized quadratic comparison and the grounded finite-cell Poincaré
inequality give
                                          AQ+ ≍ ∥dX∥2 ≍ ∥X∥2 ,
while smoothness of the finite path atlas and the fact that every retained plaquette word equals the
identity on the flat set give DQ + TQ ≤ C∥X∥2 . This proves the retention inequality on one neighborhood
of the flat set.
   Remove a smaller such neighborhood. On the resulting compact set, AQ+ has a strictly positive
minimum and the smoothly extended DQ is bounded, so their quotient is bounded there as well. This

bulk and aligned-reflection templates proves the stated uniformity.

  Choose smooth decreasing cutoffs γD , γ0 , γ1 ∈ [0, 1] with γ0 ≤ γ1 and

Lemma 3.5 (Charged support of every irregular label). After fixing 0 < χ ≤ χ0 with χ20 ≤ 4cch , every
curvature, collar, or outer label is supported on

                                                 AQ+ (U ) ≥ c0 χ2

for one fixed c0 > 0. Every derivative of a splice cutoff through order 2sG is supported on the same
charged event and has a fixed-template polynomial bound in χ−1 and the fixed chart thresholds.

Proof. On the support of 1 − γD , either DQ ≥ χ2 /4 or a derivative of γD is present. The retention
inequality gives AQ+ ≥ χ2 /(4Cret ). On the collar and outer supports, or on a derivative of γ0 or γ1 , one

gives a fixed positive action charge, which dominates c0 χ2 for χ ≤ χ0 . The derivative bounds through
order 2sG follow from the fixed smooth cutoffs, Lemma 3.1, and the finite cell atlas; differentiation changes
neither transition support nor action charge.

3.4    Component plateau and denominator

depth, wrapping, and reflected template, such that the exact fiber over (W, η) contains an analytic section
sX (W, η) satisfying
                           ∥sX (W, η)∥∞ ≤ Ctr Rg ,      AI(X) (sX ) ≤ Ctr uRg2 |X|.

                                                          16
Proof. Use the model-specific normal coordinates of Proposition 2.9 on every fixed component cell. Start
from the canonical weak prolongation of W : split each coarse logarithm equally between the two straight
fine links, set transverse details to zero, and interpolate across the fixed-width boundary templates to the
actual weak ring datum η. This gives a globally consistent configuration with the displayed pointwise
and action bounds.
   The component-owned anchor problem is a principal subproblem of the simultaneous selected-anchor
map. Theorem 3.3 supplies a uniform lower Lipschitz bound and inverse derivative with the ring anchors
fixed. It therefore gives a unique component anchor correction with constants independent of shape and
topology. Fresh details√are the remaining fiber coordinates. The finite incidence and inverse bounds show
that a ball of radius c u remains inside the common coordinate tube and on the same exact fiber.

Lemma 3.19 (Reference trial plateau). There are fixed d0 < ∞, c, C > 0 such that
                                                   h             2
                                                                         i|X|
                               eb0 qRX (η) GX (η) ≥ cud0 /2 e−C(Rg +1+r)      .

Proof. Integrate only over the plateau ball. Its number of real coordinates is at most d0 |X|, hence its
product volume is bounded below by (cud0 /2 )|X| . On that ball the Wilson action divided by u costs at
most CRg2 |X|. Every fixed Haar, coordinate, coarea, determinant, and anchor-return factor is bounded
above and below on the common finite atlas. The reproduced regular interaction and the cutoff-free
boundary reserve of the exterior-tube theorem cost at most

                                         C(1 + r)|X| + b0 qRX (η).

All good cutoffs equal one by Lemma 3.18. Multiplying the bounds and moving the exterior regulator to
the left gives the result.

Proof. The based background-slice map is a finite-dimensional analytic diffeomorphism on the        √ common

                                                  √          gauge-coordinate, and interaction factors gives
three derivatives in u uniformly; odd powers of u vanish after the signed-hypercubic orbit average. This
proves the C 3 expansion and the unit tangent. Every interaction term in R has vanishing extracted scalar
and complete retained two-jet, so it cannot alter the linear term in (8).
   Two local slice choices are joined by a finite interpolation of background-covariant gauge fermions.
The variation is BRS exact. The SI–6 counterterm classification and the supportwise SI–7M estimate of
Lemma 5.14 show that the change is a finite CR3 scheme term with unit tangent.
   For a cutoff interpolation, differentiate − log χt e−Sµ J∆ before taking the two background-amplitude
marks and the fixed projector P⋆ . This gives exactly ϵχ,t . The factor χ̇t is supported in the annulus between
the smaller and larger weak tubes. After exact label recombination, every numerator term is therefore
a charged U4 activity; a background or coupling derivative may hit the action, the coarea/Jacobian
factor, or the determinant; the fixed factor χ̇t keeps every term on the same charged annular support.
The differentiated U4 total-variation row, through the three coupling and two background derivatives
displayed in the statement, bounds the numerator by the positive denominator times Ck,α u−pk,α e−c/u .
The good-cutoff plateau and its trial-ball lower bound make this ratio uniform in t, volume, cutoff, and
preceding weak depth. Integrating in t proves that changing χ adds only the printed C 3 -flat scheme term.
The same U4 row gives the charged complement bound. Such terms are C 3 -flat, not analytic, and this
finite-order regularity is all that is consumed below.

                                             hj+1 ◦ Fj = Σj ◦ hj

through second order. If the quadratic coefficients of hj , hj+1 , and Σj are aj , aj+1 , and bj , respectively,

N ≥ 1, is relatively compact in Ba′ ,N −1 .

  For |X| ≤ L, only finitely many rooted support shapes occur. On each shape, the C N (KX ) bound is

                                           C N (KX ) ⋐ C N −1 (KX )

Theorem 5.19 (Exact fiber-chart invariance). Let Ψ1 (w, z1 ) and Ψ2 (w, z2 ) be two exact C 1 fiber charts
for the same finite-dimensional submersion B(U ) = w. Suppose their images agree on the integration
region and their transition map is orientation preserving. Then, for every integrable density F ,
                      Z                                   Z
                         F (Ψ1 (w, z1 )) J1 (w, z1 ) dz1 = F (Ψ2 (w, z2 )) J2 (w, z2 ) dz2 .

Consequently the normalized conditional expectation, its connected logarithm, and every background
derivative of the complete local standard-scheme coordinate and its quadratic coefficient are independent
of the chosen exact fiber chart.


                                                        48
Proof. The first identity is the ordinary change-of-variables theorem on the fiber. Division by the same
fiber mass gives equality of normalized conditional expectations. Connected logarithms are functions
of those expectations. Differentiation is permitted on any common dominated marked domain. The
determinant statement follows because the two Jacobians are related by the determinant of the transition
map, which cancels under the change of variables. It must not be inserted a second time.

variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.

direct sum.

10     Nontriviality and finite spectral window
10.1     Endpoint interpolation and reflected noncollapse
Let Yr (f ) be the tangent-normalized, Wick-centered scalar curvature source at physical scale r, with the
source root parallel transported in the normalized finite-jet frame. The test profile f is chosen once at
unit scale and dilated with the source so that its dimensionless support remains fixed and lies strictly in
positive Euclidean time.
Lemma 10.1 (Positive Gaussian OS norm). There is such a profile f for which

                                    V0 (f ) := S0 ΘYr (f ) Yr (f ) > 0.
                                                                  


The value is independent of r in the normalized frame.
Proof. At the Gaussian fixed point the centered scalar curvature composite creates a two-curvature Fock
vector. In Fourier variables its squared norm is an integral of the squared symmetrized two-particle
amplitude against the positive transverse curvature spectral density. That density is nonzero on an open
set, so choosing a positive-time profile whose Fourier transform is nonzero there gives a strictly positive
integral. Reflection positivity identifies the integral with the displayed OS form. Scale covariance of the
tangent-normalized source makes the value independent of r.

Lemma A.11 (Exact finite-depth twice-marked conditional forest). At finite directed depth M , differen-
tiate the exact factorization of Definition A.6 with respect to the two curvature roots and at most two
copies of g. The result is a finite sum of complete scale-labelled packet trees such that:
    (i) the two source leaves have one unique lowest common ancestor;
   (ii) each g mark is owned exactly once by a trajectory coordinate, a conditional logarithm, a source
        return, the terminal weak logarithm, or the compact handoff logarithm;
  (iii) every covariance edge, hard-core derivative, field factorial, regulator allocation and normalization
        contact belongs to one complete one-step packet before multiscale composition;

No infinite-depth passage is asserted in this lemma.
Proof. Apply the joint-cumulant derivative identity to every exact normalized weak conditional and, on

only finite-volume differentiability; the uniform compact marked bound is supplied later by Theorem A.20.
The one-step summed replica–BKAR theorem controls each weak packet, Lemma A.4 controls zero,
one and two g marks, and finite source transport controls the external roots. Uniqueness of the lowest
common ancestor is the ordinary tree property.

                                                              63
Lemma 6.28 (Product Fourier–Wiener calculus). For scalar f ∈ AI , convolution by f on L2 (GI ) is
trace class and
                                ∥Kf ∥S1 (L2 (GI )) = ∥f ∥AI .                               (63)
If I ⊂ I ′ , lifting f by independence of the coordinates in I ′ \ I preserves the norm. Pointwise multiplication
satisfies
                                              ∥f g∥AI ≤ ∥f ∥AI ∥g∥AI .                                       (64)
The same inequalities hold coefficientwise in the declared finite projective marked norm. Left/right
translations, inversion, and coordinate permutations are isometries.
Proof. Peter–Weyl decomposes L2 (GI ) into the blocks Vλ ⊗ Vλ∗ . Convolution has block fb(λ) ⊗ I, whose
trace norm is dλ ∥fb(λ)∥S1 ; summing gives (63). A lifted function has only the trivial Fourier block in
every added coordinate, proving isometry. The standard coefficient-product formula, complete contraction
in the unitary Clebsch–Gordan maps, and the projective trace norm give (64). The remaining symmetries
are unitary changes of Peter–Weyl blocks.

ideal generated by zα − 1 and preserves finite range, proving (148).

Proof. The source is a globally smooth fixed-range function of finitely many links. On the regular weak

derivatives enter the already established irregular activity row. Lemma 8.18 removes the vacuum Haar

with the traceless stress and scalar coupling-conjugate channels; the antisymmetric part is the already
controlled rotation/total-difference row. Every other regular term contains a second profile derivative,
two additional lattice derivatives, an equation-of-motion contact, or a nonreference root. After canonical
rescaling these give Cℓj p2 (v).

are bounded globally. Use the same regular/irregular partition and Wilson-deficit estimate as the all-stage

factors and is controlled by the same common marked-C 3 row. Exact conditional pushforward and the
joint matching connector preserve these bounds with a fixed factor. Finally Proposition 8.21 gives the
O(a2 ) replacement error on bounded geometric representatives.
