Finite incidence identities The finite clover and incidence formulas satisfy:
1. exact cancellation of the distinguished-link derivative in the two-plaquette clover;
2. exact gauge covariance under arbitrary lattice G gauge transformations;
3. midpoint/reflection cancellation showing an O(a3 ) per-link strip defect;
4. exact signed-hypercubic covariance of the linear incidence stencil;
5. the zero trace of every first nonlinear adjoint diagonal block.
These identities, together with the all-stage marked theorem and the terminal joint Haar–optical connector
established in Part III, complete the finite-cutoff source construction.

10     Nontriviality and finite spectral window
10.1     Endpoint interpolation and reflected noncollapse
Let Yr (f ) be the tangent-normalized, Wick-centered scalar curvature source at physical scale r, with the
source root parallel transported in the normalized finite-jet frame. The test profile f is chosen once at
unit scale and dilated with the source so that its dimensionless support remains fixed and lies strictly in
positive Euclidean time.
Lemma 10.1 (Positive Gaussian OS norm). There is such a profile f for which

                                    V0 (f ) := S0 ΘYr (f ) Yr (f ) > 0.
                                                                  


The value is independent of r in the normalized frame.
Proof. At the Gaussian fixed point the centered scalar curvature composite creates a two-curvature Fock
vector. In Fourier variables its squared norm is an integral of the squared symmetrized two-particle
amplitude against the positive transverse curvature spectral density. That density is nonzero on an open
set, so choosing a positive-time profile whose Fourier transform is nonzero there gives a strictly positive
integral. Reflection positivity identifies the integral with the displayed OS form. Scale covariance of the
tangent-normalized source makes the value independent of r.

4.3.3 Exact Gaussian disintegration and harmonic extension
Let C≥j be the mean-zero Feynman covariance before the scale-j block and let Bj = 12 Af,j be the
normalized linearized retained map. On the mean-zero coarse space define

                                 Cjc = Bj C≥j Bj∗ ,        Jj = C≥j Bj∗ (Cjc )−1 ,

and
                                              Cjd = C≥j − Jj Cjc Jj∗ .
All inverses are taken only on the stated mean-zero spaces; torons remain in the retained compact
background.
Theorem 4.11 (Exact linear Gaussian fiber decomposition). The operators above satisfy

                           Bj Jj = I,        Bj Cjd = 0,       C≥j = Cjd + Jj Cjc Jj∗ .

If W ∼ N (0, Cjc ) and ζ ∼ N (0, Cjd ) are independent, then

                                                  A = Jj W + ζ

has covariance C≥j and Bj A = W . Conversely, this is the exact conditional disintegration of the fine
Feynman Gaussian given the retained linear block. In the folded Fourier frame, Cjd is the pushforward of
Copt from Corollary 4.10.
Proof. These are the finite-dimensional Gaussian Schur-complement identities. Since Cjc = Bj C≥j Bj∗ ,
Bj Jj = I. Substitution gives Bj Cjd = 0 and the covariance sum. The characteristic function of Jj W + ζ is
therefore the one with covariance C≥j . The folded optical parameterization T (k) identifies the conditional
detail space with ker Af (k), and the conditional precision is the restriction of Hf to that space.

used below means the exact identity of Theorem 4.11, with Cj = Cjd and with the remaining covariance
embedded by Jj before the canonical identification. It is not a declaration of an unconstructed translation-
invariant product slicing.

4.3.4 Exact Wilson–Haar factorization in the nonlinear fiber chart
Let PN (W, ζ) be the exact nonlinear normal-fiber chart proved in Sections 2.4– 3.2, and let Jcoarea be its

with covariance Cjd .

Theorem 4.23 (Boundary-template Loc dichotomy). For fixed D, L, the bulk and aligned-reflection
localization atlas has finitely many rooted template classes. The preceding projector, commutator, long-row,

before reflection positivity is invoked.
Proof. The corrected half-space schedule prevents a retained path from coupling the two open half-spaces
except through the fixed plane layer. The stated path extents and the finite radius R(L) give only finitely
many rooted boundary intersections up to translation and the aligned-reflection stabilizer. Construct
LocD templatewise and average over that stabilizer. Taking the maximum of finitely many constants
proves the uniform estimate.
   The labelled weak/collar/outer densities recombine pointwise before the Osterwalder–Schrader form is
formed, so localization does not assert reflection positivity of an individual slice or label.

   For U2, the centered analytic graph theorem gives the full remainder with an initially named graph

The two background marks create external legs and do not differentiate external momentum, so they do
not lower these orders. A mixed forest contraction is loop-free. A pure-Wilson contraction produces only
a standard dimension-four background-field vertex, while every quotient that retains the stable insertion
retains its degree-six marked vertex.
   Consequently every subtraction-generated exceptional configuration is covered by the SI–7M forest
theorem, not estimated as a separated Taylor piece. In particular, for the one-loop two-point classes the
complete list of soft degrees is

                                   ω vector bubble = 4 − 2 · 2 + 1 + 1 = 2,
                                   ω ghost bubble = 4 − 2 · 2 + 1 + 1 = 2,
                                  ω seagull/tadpole = 4 − 2 = 2.

These values also cover the exceptional zero-momentum configurations created inside the Lowenstein
operators. A loop-bearing marked quotient has at least two additional infrared degrees. Thus every complete
packet has strictly positive lower infrared degree in every soft subspace.


                                                      45
   The pure-Wilson ultraviolet degrees are the SI–6 degrees. By the one-cycle ledger, a mixed contraction
consumes the loop; otherwise the quotient retains a degree-six marked kernel and has ultraviolet excess at
least two. Hence all overall and proper-quotient ultraviolet degrees required by SI–7 are admissible. After
the finite SI–7M sector decomposition, uniformly in the periodic side M and through the two marks, the
grouped packet obeys
                                                       
                                       X
            RGLZ
                 (q, 1; k) ≤ C PX 1 +     dT4 (k, ξ)−2  ,     PX ≤ C(1 + diam X)p ∥HX ∥a;3,2 ,       (23)
                                      ξ∈SG

with the evident omission of PX for a pure-Wilson packet and with a finite soft set SG . A radius-r shell
contains O(M 4 r4 ) lattice momenta, so (23) contributes O(r2 ) after M −4 normalization. The dyadic shell
sum is uniform in M ; deleting a grounded zero mode only decreases it. SI–7 and SI–7M therefore give the
cutoff and s ↑ 1 limits of the complete packet, and the unused rooted support exponent absorbs PX before
the thermodynamic sum.
Proof. The quadratic, cubic, quartic, gauge-fixing, and ghost rows are the finite Taylor rows of the local
Wilson background action and based Faddeev–Popov density. Their derivative and leg counts give the
first four rows of the table. The marked row is Lemma 5.9; the one-cycle classification in Lemma 5.10
shows that forest reduction either removes the loop or leaves that row intact. Equation (21) then proves
the displayed infrared ledger, including the simultaneous two-line soft configuration at a subtraction point.
This verifies the general vertex hypothesis of SI–7M for the original graph and all reduced vertices. The
SI–7M complete-forest conclusion supplies the grouped sector majorant; the explicit degree two gives the
exponent in (23). The shell count and the strict rooted reserve prove the stated uniform limits.
Lemma 5.13 (Finite-support marked power-counting consequence). Let HX satisfy (19). For a diam X ≤
1, the BPHZL-subtracted one-loop nonexceptional background packet has the form
                    (2)
                   IX (a, q⋆ ) = AX (q⋆ ) + O a2 | log a| C(1 + diam X)q1 ∥HX ∥a;3,2 ,         (24)
                                                         

                                       (2)
where AX (q⋆ ) is finite and [log a] P⋆ IX = 0. Supports with a diam X > 1 have rooted total O(aN ) for
every fixed N .
Proof. Fix one support and q ∈ Q⋆ , keeping background, quantum, and ghost momenta separate. Apply
the complete SI–6B/SI–7M packet and the graph classification of Lemma 5.10. Lemma 5.12 verifies, for
every complete packet and through the two consumed background marks, lattice periodicity, denominator
scaling, numerator smoothness, all overall and proper-subgraph degrees, the pure-Wilson/loop-free-
local/loop-bearing marked trichotomy, support-polynomial control, and uniform rooted domination.
SI–7M is therefore applicable to every complete loop-bearing post-contraction packet. Pure-Wilson
proper divergent subgraphs are removed in the SI–6 scheme, while every quotient that still contains
the distinguished stable insertion has irrelevant excess at least two. Lemma 5.9 gives a finite sum of
two-quantum-leg symbols whose complete marked local Taylor jet starts at engineering degree d ≥ 6. The
complete marked Taylor polynomial of the insertion through degree four is zero by (19). Subtractions
may produce local power rows but cannot restore a marginal input exponent; the remaining symbol
carries ad−4 .
   On each fixed unit loop-momentum annulus, the verified SI–7/SI–7M hypotheses give the uniform
marked bound used below. Write the ultraviolet annuli as |k| ≍ 2j with 0 ≤ j ≤ J(a) = O(| log a|). A
marked component of engineering degree d contributes to P⋆ with an explicit ad−4 and an annulus row
bounded by C2(d−4)j . If d > 4, the annulus sum is geometric:
                                                   J(a)
                                                   X
                                         a   d−4
                                                          2(d−4)j = O(1),
                                                   j=0

so it produces a finite local scheme row and no logarithm. A logarithm occurs only at the marginal
exponent d − 4 = 0. Higher external-momentum Taylor coefficients can lower the annulus exponent to
zero, but then they retain the explicit ad−4 ; for d ≥ 6 their logarithmic row is O(a2 | log a|). Hence an
unsuppressed P⋆ log a term would require a nonzero marked degree-four input jet, which is absent. No
assertion that a redundant dimension-four insertion is annihilated support by support is used: such rows
have already been extracted and are not present in HX .
   Finite-support multiplicity is polynomial in diam X; for a diam X > 1, the strict support reserve
dominates every prescribed power of a.

xponential, multiplication, inversion,
logarithm, and Karcher-mean maps defining every template extend holomorphically from Tη0 to Tη1 .
   Write the canonically rescaled local block as

                             B(x) = Lx + N (x),       N (0) = 0,             DN (0) = 0.

For every exponential kernel rate 0 < m < mP and r = 2, 3, there is a finite constant

                                             CN,r (m; η0 , η1 ) < ∞

such that
                                    sup ∥Dr N (x)∥Km ≤ CN,r (m; η0 , η1 ),
                                   x∈Tη0

uniformly under the standing convention. The same constants, enlarged by a finite maximum, serve the
aligned-reflection templates.
Proof. For one retained cell the block depends on finitely many g coordinates. The exponential and
multiplication maps are entire, while inversion and the principal logarithm are holomorphic on a fixed
neighborhood of the identity. At the flat field the Karcher critical point has positive Hessian on the
retained tangent space; the analytic implicit-function theorem therefore supplies a unique holomorphic
Karcher mean on a fixed complex neighborhood. The path family and the reflected template family are
finite, so one may choose common nested radii 0 < η0 < η1 for all of them. On the larger finite-dimensional
complex polydisc, every elementary map and their finite compositions are holomorphic and uniformly
bounded. Cauchy’s integral estimate on the smaller polydisc gives uniform derivative bounds through
order three with denominator powers of η1 − η0 .
   The global block is obtained by translating this fixed finite cell. Each output row meets only a fixed
number of input cells, and each input column enters only a fixed number of output cells. Multiplying the
finite-cell Cauchy bounds by these fixed incidence numbers gives the two-sided exponentially weighted
Schur-kernel bounds. These incidence numbers do not depend on the total volume or preceding weak
depth. The aligned-reflection family is finite, so one takes a finite maximum.

Let TQ be a fixed smooth invariant dispersion function formed from the squared relative-loop distances of
the forty-nine path inputs. It vanishes on the flat set, its small sublevel set lies inside the common Karcher
chart, and it uses only one fixed enlarged-cell template. On that sublevel set the retained holonomies
Wp′ are defined by the local Karcher atlas. Multiply the displayed retained deficit by a fixed chart cutoff
that is one on a prescribed smaller Karcher sublevel and zero before the edge of the larger Karcher tube;
this gives a smooth invariant extension to the complete finite-cell configuration space. The threshold tch
below is chosen so that the entire curvature-label support lies where this cutoff equals one.
Lemma 3.1 (Representation-normalized charge and derivative ledger). For every fixed G, faithful ρ,
and integer 0 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3, there are finite constants depending only on the fixed cell atlas such that

                                wG,ρ (U ) ≥ 0,      wG,ρ (U ) = 0 ⇐⇒ U = e,

                             cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2       (∥X∥ ≤ r0,G ),
and
             ∥DwG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG wG,ρ (U )1/2 ,       ∥Dr wG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG,r     (2 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3).
The same bounds, with finite template constants, hold after composition with every Wilson word, retained
plaquette word, reflected word, or seam word used below.
Proof. Nonnegativity and the zero set are Proposition 1.2. In the chosen invariant metric the Hessian of
wG,ρ at the identity is positive definite; Taylor’s theorem gives the two-sided quadratic comparison. The
first-derivative estimate follows from that comparison on the identity tube. On its compact complement
wG,ρ has a positive minimum while its derivative is bounded, so the same inequality holds globally after
enlarging the constant. All higher derivatives are bounded because wG,ρ is a smooth matrix coefficient
on compact G. Every word in the fixed path atlas is a finite composition of multiplication and inversion,
so the chain rule gives the asserted finite derivative bounds.

Let TQ be a fixed smooth invariant dispersion function formed from the squared relative-loop distances of
the forty-nine path inputs. It vanishes on the flat set, its small sublevel set lies inside the common Karcher
chart, and it uses only one fixed enlarged-cell template. On that sublevel set the retained holonomies
Wp′ are defined by the local Karcher atlas. Multiply the displayed retained deficit by a fixed chart cutoff
that is one on a prescribed smaller Karcher sublevel and zero before the edge of the larger Karcher tube;
this gives a smooth invariant extension to the complete finite-cell configuration space. The threshold tch
below is chosen so that the entire curvature-label support lies where this cutoff equals one.
Lemma 3.1 (Representation-normalized charge and derivative ledger). For every fixed G, faithful ρ,
and integer 0 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3, there are finite constants depending only on the fixed cell atlas such that

                                wG,ρ (U ) ≥ 0,      wG,ρ (U ) = 0 ⇐⇒ U = e,

                             cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2       (∥X∥ ≤ r0,G ),
and
             ∥DwG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG wG,ρ (U )1/2 ,       ∥Dr wG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG,r     (2 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3).
The same bounds, with finite template constants, hold after composition with every Wilson word, retained
plaquette word, reflected word, or seam word used below.
Proof. Nonnegativity and the zero set are Proposition 1.2. In the chosen invariant metric the Hessian of
wG,ρ at the identity is positive definite; Taylor’s theorem gives the two-sided quadratic comparison. The
first-derivative estimate follows from that comparison on the identity tube. On its compact complement
wG,ρ has a positive minimum while its derivative is bounded, so the same inequality holds globally after
enlarging the constant. All higher derivatives are bounded because wG,ρ is a smooth matrix coefficient
on compact G. Every word in the fixed path atlas is a finite composition of multiplication and inversion,
so the chain rule gives the asserted finite derivative bounds.

Gauge-covariant local jet projector. For a rooted support Y , choose the canonical root cell r(Y ).

    Let JD (Y ) be the finite-dimensional space of gauge-complete, signed-hypercubic covariant, Wick-local

Lemma 4.20 (Discrete covariant localization). There is a projector

                                          LocD,Y : FY 7−→ JD (Y )

with the following properties.
1. Loc2D,Y = LocD,Y , and it is exact on JD (Y ).
2. It commutes with gauge transformations, signed-hypercubic symmetries, and aligned reflection.
3. For 0 ≤ m ≤ 3,
                                ∥ LocD,Y FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD ∥FY ∥[m] .
4. If LocD,Y FY = 0, then after canonical scale-L rescaling,

                                 ∥TL FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD L3−D ∥FY ∥[m] .

  The estimate includes both the nonlocal output and its newly generated local projection:

                ∥ LocD TL FY ∥[m] + ∥(1 − LocD )TL FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD L3−D ∥FY ∥[m] .

Proof. On each finite local template, take the gauge-complete Taylor expansion in normal coordinates
and scale-dependent Wick monomials, transport all coefficients to the canonical root, and average over
the finite stabilizer. The dual coefficient functionals use finitely many derivatives and finite-difference


                                                     32
moments. Transport along a tree of radius τ (Y ) costs at most a polynomial in 1 + τ (Y ), proving the
third row.
   If LocD,Y FY = 0, every declared Taylor coefficient through degree D vanishes. The integral form of
the covariant Taylor remainder begins at degree D + 1. Canonical rescaling gives one root-preimage factor
L4 and L−(D+1) from the smallest omitted engineering degree. Thus the gain is

                                              L4−(D+1) = L3−D .

The regular linearized block kernel is analytic on the fixed tube and bounded through the three active
marked rows, so integration and the local coefficient functionals preserve this remainder estimate. Applying
the projector after integration proves the displayed localization-commutator bound; in particular, a
remainder cannot feed the finite local block without paying the same L3−D factor.

Lemma A.12 (Top marked ancestor and complete-packet shell). In a packet tree from Lemma A.11,
contract every maximal descendant subtree carrying no distinguished label into its exact parent-normalized
decoration, and suppress every maximal unary chain into its exact ordered transfer product. The distin-
guished labels are the two curvature roots and the zero, one or two active g marks. They have a unique
lowest common ancestor v⊤ ; let j be its conditional scale. Call v⊤ the top marked ancestor.
    All vertices strictly above v⊤ are unmarked. The complete coefficient created at v⊤ includes every
contracted side decoration with its canonical reported support. Decompose these whole coefficients by their
full reported-support shell k using a scalar translation- and reflection-invariant selector. The selected
class is therefore a complete BRS/reflection packet with every denominator and normalization contact
still inside it.

                                                                 k−j
                                            C(1 + 2k−j )q e−c2         .                                   (208)

If j > k + C0 , the full support lies inside a fixed enlargement of one scale-j cell and no spatial connector
estimate is needed.

Proof. Contraction of an unlabelled descendant is its exact conditional value, so it retains its canonical
reported support. Suppression of a unary chain is the exact ordered transfer product. After these
operations, a tree with at most four distinguished leaves has only a fixed number of essential branching
edges, while all arbitrarily long unary portions are already contained in the parity transfer product. The
unique lowest common ancestor of all labels is an ordinary tree invariant. If a g derivative lands on a
coarser conditional map, its mark is attached at that map and therefore moves v⊤ to that scale. Hence
no marked vertex can occur above v⊤ .
   The shell selector is applied only after the full packet at v⊤ has been collected. A remote unmarked
side decoration is not cut from its parent and is not asked to carry an external Taylor factor. If exact
localization turns its contribution into a smaller-hull contact, that term is already in the finite contact block
and is not a shell-k packet. Otherwise its canonical reported support remains part of the whole packet.
When that support spans 2k−j scale-j blocks, the strict rooted-support norm and the weak connector
theorem give (208), including covariance, hard-core and mixed alternatives. The exact Loc/collect identity
of Theorem 5.8 ensures that this dichotomy is made before the stable packet is declared.

   If j > k + C0 , no connector is needed and 2−2j ≤ C2−2k 2−2(j−k−C0 ) . The two tails in (213) are
respectively supergeometric and geometric. A fixed defect changes only the finite root norm.
Lemma A.15 (Complete root-separating bridge cancellation). At any finite weak terminal logarithm,
after complete BRS/reflection, regular/irregular and normalization recombination, the complete sum of
a covariance class possessing a canonically selected root-separating bridge is zero before absolute values.
The identity remains true with zero, one or two already assigned g marks.
Proof. Contract the maximal two-edge-connected blocks and select the first bridge on the bridge-tree
path leaving the negative-time root. Deleting that bridge leaves two complete connected sides and gives
the exact coefficientwise factorization
                                  X             X
                                        w(G) =       Ccc′ (x, y)Lc (x)Rc′ (y).
                               G: E⋆ ={e}           x,y,c,c′

Packet completion precedes the bridge selection, so denominator derivatives, localization contacts, source-
motion contacts and off-path branches remain inside L and R. Each side contains one scalar curvature
root and one open adjoint leg. Global color invariance makes both one-leg packets vanish. The identity
holds for the whole C 2 family in g, hence also after any distribution of at most two g marks.
   For one crossing cell with first-crossing depth N , put
                                                               √
                             ΛN,m := Cm (N + 2)Pm eCm           N +1
                                                                       ,   m = 0, 1, 2.                  (214)

order: the derivative is already taken before the partition is inserted. Hence the terms ∂gj χλQ never arise,

                                                       108
Theorem A.23 (Geometric twice-marked conditional-packet shell theorem). Fix a finite directed depth M
and one crossing cell from Definition A.6. Let FM,k (g) be the cellwise complete globally contact-subtracted
reported-support shell from Lemma A.5, including all regular and irregular activities, covariance and
hard-core connections, determinant and coarea rows, source motion and normalization contacts. The
same notation is used with one fixed finite defect root. There are finite Pm , Cm , independent of M and of
the cell, such that, for m = 0, 1, 2,
                                                                        √
                               |∂gm FM,k (g)| ≤ Cm (k + 2)Pm 2−2k eCm       k+1
                                                                                  .                   (241)

The cellwise shell series and its first two g derivatives converge with one uniform majorant in cutoff,
volume, preceding weak depth and the chosen weak scale r. Consequently the derivative bounds for the
total separated functional extend to crossing-cell boundaries by finite-volume C 2 continuity.

Proof. Work first at finite directed depth M and inside one crossing cell of Definition A.6. Apply the
exact marked conditional forest. If the top marked ancestor is a weak conditional vertex of scale j,
Lemma A.14 gives the complete row at that scale. Every later weak transfer before the selected terminal
factor is unmarked, and Theorem A.13 contracts the entire complete rooted packet in one norm. A
coupling mark after the two source roots have joined is not omitted: it becomes another distinguished
leaf and moves the top marked ancestor to its own scale.
   If the top marked ancestor is the selected terminal factor, apply Theorem A.22. On a crossing cell
this is the complete compact handoff packet, with the two differences created at the first-crossing scale
and the remaining separation paid by the joint Haar–optical connector. On the no-crossing cell it is
the finite-depth weak terminal packet on the fixed coarse torus. The latter tends to zero through two
g derivatives as M → ∞. Thus no bridge-free expansion of the residual grounded covariance and no
massless Schur row is required.
   Sum the weak top scales √
                               j using (213) and add the uniform terminal row (240). The result is a
polynomial times 2−2k eC k , independent of M and of the crossing cell. The sum of the shell bounds
is uniform up to the closure of each crossing cell. The cellwise shell series and its first two derivatives
therefore pass to the cutoff and volume limits uniformly on that closed cell. At a shared boundary the
original total finite-volume normalized functional is C 2 by Lemma 10.3; equality of the two one-sided
total derivatives passes to the limit. Thus no individual shell coefficients are compared and the crossing
index is never differentiated. At g = 0 use the no-crossing cell for every M and let M → ∞.
   The shell majorant is summable in k. Dominated convergence therefore permits the directed-depth,
volume and ultraviolet limits and two g derivatives, also with one fixed defect root.
Corollary A.24 (Endpoint-safe even interpolation). For the fixed principal reflected form and its version
with one finite defect root,

                                  d2 b
                             sup      Q (g) ≤ Cf ,
                                     2 r
                                                                b ′ (g)| ≤ Cf g.
                                                           sup |Q r                                   (242)
                           0≤g≤gr dg                      0≤g≤gr

The constants are uniform in cutoff, volume, preceding weak depth, and the chosen weak scale r. No
matching mass is used at g = 0.
Proof. This is (170). The master theorem obtains the bound from the complete contact block plus the
uniformly summable separated packet-shell series, including one fixed defect root; exact evenness supplies
b ′r (0) = 0. No crossing index is differentiated and no massless terminal Schur row is used.
Q
